# Import licensing policy for July-December 1953 for newcomers.

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE AND INDUSTRY PUBLIC NOTICES IMPORT TRADE CONTROL New Delhi, the 24th June 1953.

SUBJECT:—Import licensing policy for July-December 1953 for newcomers.

No. 93-ITC(P.N.)/53—The following decision of the Government of India regarding the issue of licences to newcomers for the import of certain articles for the licensing period July-December 1953 are published for general information:—

2. The list of items for which licences will be given to newcomers is shown in the list attached to this Public Notice.

3. Persons or firms who have been dealing in the internal trade of the particular commodity for a minimum period of two consecutive years ending 31st March 1953 and who are not eligible to qualify as established importers or actual users can submit applications for newcomer licences.

4. Established importers who do not wish to claim their quota as such may apply as newcomers. Such firms should furnish in addition to the evidence of their past imports, evidence of their turnover giving reasons why a licence as an established importer has not been claimed.

5. Applications should be submitted in the prescribed form together with a certificate from a Chartered Accountant showing that the applicant has been dealing in the internal trade of the country in respect of a particular item and certifying the turnover of the applicant in respect of that item during the period of two consecutive years ending 3lst March 1953. The application form as well as the auditor's certificate attached thereto given in the "Handbook" should be deemed to have been amended accordingly.

Documentary evidence should also be furnished in support of purchases of the goods concerned from importers or wholesalers.

[PART I

6. The minimum turnover which will qualify a newcomer for being considered for the grant of an import licence has been given in the attached list. This condition of minimum turnover will apply only to firms having branches or headquarters at the ports of Bombay, Calcutta and Madras.

In the case of firms with headquarters at other places and with no branches at the ports of Bombay, Calcutta and Madras, applications for import licences will be considered if the turnover does not fall below one-fourth of the minimum given in the attached list.

7. Applicants who have received licences as newcomers for the licensing period January-June 1953 need not submit fresh evidence to prove that they have been engaged in the internal trade of the country. It will be sufficient if they will attach to their applications a photostat or a certified copy of the licence received by them for the period January-June 1953. The certificate should be endorsed by a Magistrate of the First Class.

8. Applications from newcomers complete in all respects should reach the appropriate licensing authority on or before the 3lst August 1953. Incomplete applications or applications received after that date will not be considered.

9. The minimum value limit of an import licence to be granted to newcomers will be the same as prescribed in the case of established Importers. The maximum value of a licence to be issued to a newcomer will not ordinarily exceed the amount shown in the attached list against each item.

10. Government does not bind itself to- grant newcomer licences ito each applicant.

[PAHT I SEC 1] SUBJECT:—Licensing Policy for Machine Tools for the licensing period July-December 1953 No. 94-ITC(P.N.)/53.—For purposes of licensing, Machine Tools falling under Part VI of the Import Trade Control Schedule (vide Schedule A attached) are divided into two main categories, viz: —

(a) those falling within the definition of Capital Goods, and

(b) Others.

2. The rules and procedure in the case of Machine Tools falling In the category of Capital Goods have been set out in Chapter II of Plant and Machinery Handbook. The Import policy for Machine Tools which do not come under the Capital Goods Scheme is set out In the succeeding paragraphs. These licences will be issued by the Development Officer (Tools), Tools Directorate, Ministry of Commerce and Industry, New Delhi.

3. Applications will be entertained from established Importers, actual' users and newcomers.

4. Established importers will be granted general and soft currency licences on the basis of 50 per cent, of half of their best yearns imports from the dollar area and 75 per cent, of half of their best years' imports from the soft currency area respectively,

5. The list of machine tools which are manufactured in India and for which import licences will not be valid is given in Schedule B.

6. The procedure prescribed for the grant of licences to established importers in paragraphs 8 to 23 in Section I of the Book will apply mutatis mutandis to established importers of Machine tools also.

7. Applications from established importers for grant of supplementary licences will be considered ad hoc provided full justification is furnished. The last date for receipt of such applications will be 30th November 1953 and each importer will be allowed to make one such application only.

8. Actual users will be given their licences on the basis of their requirements. They should furnish full justification with particular reference to the end use of the machine tools applied for. Consumption certificates from the Directors of Industries etc. will not be necessary.

9. Licences will be issued to newcomers also. Newcomers should furnish along with their applications a certificate from a chartered accountant certifying the turnover in machine tools during the two consecutive years ending 31st March 1952. No minimum •urnover has been prescribed for eligibility to receive a licence.

[PART I Newcomers who are sole agents of manufacturers abroad should give particulars of their overseas connection including a copy of the Agreement reached between the newcomer and the manufacturer.

10. All applications whether by established importers, or by actual users or newcomers, should contain full descriptive details, of the machine tools desired to be imported. Descriptive catalogues must be sent along with the application.

11. Licences will be given for permissible types of machine toola subject to the proviso that the import of the following types will be limited to such reasonable proportion of the value of the licence aa the licensing authority may consider sufficient to meet current requirements: —

(i) Lathes.

(ii) Drilling machines,

(iii) Lathe chucks,

(iv) Machine vices,

12. Licences for machine tools will also be valid for import of their spares.

13. Licences will continue to be granted to meet specific orders, placed by the Director General of Supply and Disposals and Government Railways as heretofore.

14. The validity of licences will, in the case of machine tools required to fulfil D.G.S. & D. and Government Railways contracts, be in accordance with the delivery stipulated in the contract; in other cases licences will ordinarily have a validity of 18 monthsfrom the date of issue.

15. The limiting factor for licences for machine tools will bequantity, but if the actual price exceeds the face value of the licence by more than 5 per cent, it will be necessary to present the licence for amendment. Requests for amendment of value will be enter tained in respect of quota licences only and the increase, if allowed, will be adjusted against future quotas. In the case of licence for spares, value will be the limiting factor.

16. All applications for machine tools which do not come under the Capital Goods Scheme should be made to the Development Officer, Tools Directorate, Ministry of Commerce and Industry, New Delhi, together with relevant documents tri the prescribed form and! manner.

SCHEDULE 'B' TO MACHINE TOOLS ANNEXURE Licences for the following machine tools will not ordinarily be issued. Licences for machine tools which can be supplanted by the indigenous manufactured machines will also not ordinarily be issued. Where actual users require any of these items for their own use, the question of granting a licence can be considered provided full justification is given and it is proved that the indigenous manufacturers cannot supply the machines: —

(a) Centre Lathe.

(i) Cone pulley type up to 13" height of centre,

(ii) All geared head up to 9" height of centre,

As such, ball bearings of sizes UT 140E, UT 145E and UT 155E can only be imported within the restricted quota irrespective of the fact whether they are imported with or without the sleeves.

(it) In addition to the five makes given in tne appendices C (1), C (2) and C (3) viz., Hoffman^SKF, R & M, FBC and New Departures, ball bearings of other makes falling within the specific sizes mentioned in the appendices will also be treated as 'restricted types' and will be licensed within the restricted quota.

[PAST I APPENDIX 'D' LICENSING OP SMALL TOOLS FALLING UNDER SERIAL NOS. 21(1), 20(2), 20(3) AND 20(4) OF PART II OF THE IMPORT TRADE CONTROL SCHEDULE FOR THE PERIOD JULY-DECEMBER, 1953.

Licences for Small Tools namely metal working tools, wood working tools, hand tools and twist drills and reamers falling under Serial Nos. 20(1), 20(2), 20(3) and 20(4) of Part II of the I.T.C.

Schedule will be granted by the Development Officer (Tools), Tools Directorate, Ministry of Commerce and Industry, Shahjahan Road, New Delhi.

2. Applications will be entertained from Established Importers, Actual Users and New Comers.

3. The quota percentages for established importers are given in the annexed Schedule 'A'. The list of small tools which are manufactured in India and for which import licences will not be valid is given in Schedule 'B\

4. The procedure prescribed for the grant of licences to established importers in paragraphs 8 to 23 of Section I of the Book will apply mutatis mutandis to established importers of small tools also.

5. The validity of the licences will be 12 months from the date of issue and the limiting factor for purposes of clearance from Customs will be the quantity in the case of small tools (Pt. 11/20) but if the actual price exceeds the face value of the licence by more than 5 per cent., it will be necessary to present the licences for amendment. The increase in value will be adjusted against future quotas of the firm, in bona fide cases of increase in price, freight, insurance and shipping charges.

6. Actual users will be given their licences for a period not exceeding six months, provided the articles are not banned and are not normally available from the established importers or from indigenous sources. Only one application should be submitted as actual user for each currency area during one period. Consumption certificates from the Directors of Industries and Development Officers will not be necessary. The new comers should furnish a certificate from a chartered accountant certifying the turnover in small tools during the two consecutive years ending 31st March 1952. No minimum turnover has been prescribed for eligibility to receive a licence. New comers who are sole agents of manufacturers abroad should give particulars of their overseas connections, including a copy of the Agreement reached between new comer and the manufacturer. ' ,

7. FU11 description of the stores, indicating also the sizes of the tools, quantities of each etc. should be given in the application.

Vague descriptions like cutting tools, wood working tools etc. will not be enough.

8. All applications for small tools should be made to the Development Officer (Tools), Tools Directorate, Ministry of Commerce and Industry, Shahjahan Road, New Delhi, together with relevant documents in the prescribed form and manner.

Schedule A—contd.

APPENDIX G LICENCING POLICY FOR IMPORTS OF MINERAL OILS, KEROSENE, MOTOR SPIRIT, GREASES AND LUBRICATING OILS, FALLING UNDER SERIAL Nos. 105 AND 106 OF PART IV AND 8, 17, 18, 19 AND 20 OF PART V OF THE IMPORT TRADE CONTROL SCHEDULE.

1. Import of Mineral Oils falling under S. Nos. 105 and 106 of Part IV and Kerosene and Motor Spirit falling under S. Nos. 1Q and 19 of Part V of the I.T.C. Schedule.

Licensing will be granted only to oil companies importing the article in bulk without packing.

2. Impart of Greases and petroleum jellies etc. falling under Serial Number 8 of Part V of the I.T.C. Schedule

(a) Special greases and lubricants for chlorine, caustic soda and sulphuric acid service are included in O.G.L. XXIX.

(b) No licences will be granted for import of paraffin wax from any source.

(c) 8-V "For other greases and petroleum jellies, licences will be issued on a quota basis which will be fixed on the basis of past imports of these products. The best year selected should be the same as for general lubricating oils falling under Serial No. 20, Part V. The importer will, however, have an option to import either greases or lubricating oils of viscosity more than 90 Sees, for an equivalent amount".

3. Import of Mineral Oils not otherwise specified falling under Serial Number 17 of Part V of the I.T.C. Schedule

(a) For import of transformer oils, Switch oils and insulating oils falling under S. No. 17 of Part V of the I.T.C.

Schedule separate applications should be submitted for each of the items giving full information in respect of the following points viz:

(1) the essentiality and the purpose for which the oil will be used (2) the names of the parties to whom the oil will be supplied together -with the requirements of each party, or the quantities supplied to each party during the previous half year and (3) in the cases of imports ex-U.S,A. the justification for their demand giving technical reasons why oils available ex-U.K. are unsuitable for their requirements, General Licences for Transformer Oils, Switch Oil & Insulating Oil will be granted to Established Importers on the basis of a quota of 100 per cent, of half of best year's imports for these grades only from dollar area and soft currency licences on the basis of 200 per cent, of half of best year's imports for theso items from Soft Currency Area. Tn addition, soft currency licences will also be granted at 30 per cent, of half of best year's imports of these oils from dollar area.

Licences for Transformer Oil will be valid for the imDort of all types of Transformer Oils conforming to A.S.T.M. and B, S, specifications.

S04

[PART I APPENDIX G—contd.

(b) The policy with regard to the import of Medicinal Liquid paraffin to USP/BP specifications has been separately given in Appendix 'A' to this Book.

Of all the other oils, falling under this Serial No. licences will be granted only for the following on a quota basis: —

(1) Cutting Oils, (2) Mineral and Colza Oils, (3) Shock Absorber Oils, (4) Flushing Oils, (5) Penetrating Oils,

(6) Leather Oils, (7) Textile Finishing Oils,, (8) Textile Fibre Oils, (9) Heat Transfer Oils, (10) Wire rope compounds and Bituminous Gear Lubricants, (11) Aviation Specialities, (12) Corrosion Preventives, (13) Impregnating Oil for electrical paper and board insulators,

(14) Light process oil for light coloured rubber goods,

(15) Mineral oil for manufacture of insecticides, (16) Special paper softening oil for tissues and speciality papers, (17) Quenching Oils, (18) Special graphited oil for lubrication of glass moulds, (19) Aromatic Mineral Oils-Rubber Plasticisers, (20) Mineral Oil Emulsions Mould Lublicants, (21) Batching Oil for fibres, (22) Mineral Hydrocarbon oil used as a pour point depressant, (23) Mineral Oils used as additive for lubricating oils, (24) Fuel Oils, (25) Oils for vacuum work in laboratory equipment. (26) Dust preventives.

General licences will be granted to established importers on the basis of a quota of 100 per cent, of half of best year's imports from dollar area and soft currency licences on the basis of 200 per cent, of half of best year's imports from soft currency area. In addition, soft currency licences will also be granted at 30 per cent, of half of best year's imports from dollar area.

Issue of import licences for Mineral oil not otherwise specified falling under Serial No. 17 of Part V of the I.T.C. Schedule is also subject to such conditions as may be imposed and such instructions as may be issued from time to time by the Petroleum Division of the Ministry of W.H. & S.

4. Import of lubricating oils falling under Serial No. 20 of Part V 'of the I.T.C. Schedule Separate applications should be submitted for oils having viscosity over 90 seconds (Redwood 1 at 140° F) and oils of viscosities 90 seconds (Redwood 1 at 140° F) and less.

Applications for the second category i.e., for the light lubricating oils rtf viscosities 90 seconds and less must mention the enduse giving quantities against each enduse.

In order to give importers a certain amount of flexibility of programme, the licences to be issued for the light viscosity oils will not stipulate any viscosity limit, as there is no objection to importers bringing in some high viscosity oils against these licences, the object being solely to restrict the import of light viscosity oils. The licences to be issued for the other category i.e., lubricating oils with a viscosity over 90 seconds (Redwood 1 at 140° F) will contain a condition that the oils to be imported against the licences should have viscosity over 90 seconds.

APPENDIX G—contd.

General licences will be granted to established importers on the basis of a quota of 100 per cent, of half Df best year's imports from dollar area and soft currency licences on the basis of 200 per cent, of naif of best years impoits from soft currency area. In addition, soft currency licences will also be granted at 30 per cent, of half of best year's import^ from dollar area. The quota will be a combined one for lubricating oils and greases.

5. N J licences will be granted for import of white oil from any source and past imports of white oils will not be taken into account for lubricating oils and greases.

6. Separate applications should be submitted to the Chief Controller of Imports, New Delhi as follows: —

1. S. Nos. 105, 106—Part IV of the Schedule, and S. Nos. 18, 19—

Part V of the Schedule: —

Oil Companies should apply for bulk licences.

II. S. No. 8—Part V of the Schedule:—(Combined quota with Serial No. 20—V vide para. 2(c).

For Greases and Petroleum jellies.

III. S. No. 17—Part V of the Schedule: —

(i) For Transformer.

(ii) Switch.

(iii) Insulating Oils.

(iv) For the remaining items.

N.B.—(Even though separate applications are to be made for the different sub-items, applicants should note that all applications for quota licences should be made on the same day and in the same cover, furnishing cross-references regarding all the applications made by them for this S. No. as a whole).

IV. S. No. 20—Part V of the Schedule: —

(i) For lubricating oils of viscosities over 90 seconds (Redwood 1 at 140" F).

(ii) For lubricating oils having viscosities 90 seconds and less (Redwood 1 at 140° ¥) giving the end-uses and quantities against each item.

(Note—Combined quota 8—V vide para. 2/C).

[PART I APPENDIX H LIST OF DRUGS AND MEPICINES AND PHARMACEUTICAL CHEMICALS FALLING UNDER SERIAL NOS. 87 AND 109 OF PART IV FOR THE IMPORT OF WHICH THE LICENSING POLICY INDICATED BELOW WILL BE FOLLOWED.

General licences will be granted on a quota of 75 per cent, of half of best year's imports of all drugs and medicines falling under S. Nos. 87 and 109 Part IV whether included in list I or not but excluding those specifically mentioned in paras. 4 to 12 below.

Not more than one tenth of the face value of the General licence granted under this quota can be utilised for the import of drugs and medicines not covered by list I but excluding those specifically mentioned in paras. 4 to 12 below. Import of Patent or Proprietary medicines as defined in Clause (d) of Section III of the Drugs Act 1940 (XXIII of 1940) not containing spirit and not otherwise specified, can however be effected upto twenty per cent, of the face value of the licences granted under this quota.

2. Soft Currency licences will be granted on 100 per cent, of half of best year's imports of all drugs and medicines falling under S. Nos. 87 and 109 Part IV, whether included in list I or not but excluding those specifically mentioned in paras. 4 to 12 below. Not more than one-tenth of the face value of the soft currency licence granted under this quota can be utilised for the import of drugs and medicines not covered by list I but excluding those specifically mentioned in paras. 4 to 12 below. Import of Patent or proprietary medicines as defined in Clause (d) of Section m of the Drugs Act 1940 (XXITI of 1940) not containing spirit and not otherwise specified, can however be effected upto twenty per cent, of the face value of the licences granted under this quota. 3 Licences granted under this procedure will not be valid for the import of drugs and medicines shown in List II, nor for the drugs and medicines mentioned In paras. 4 to 12 below. 4 Homoeopathic Medicines.—General and Soft Currency licences for Homoeopathic medicines will be granted separately on a quota of 200 per cent of half of best year's imports of Homoeopathic medicine? from dollar and soft currency areas respectively. The licences will be valid for the import of Homoeopellets and Lactose which is used as a base for the preparation of Homoeopathic medicines Licences granted for imports from soft currency area can also be utilised for the import of special types of phials which are used for bottling and storing Homoeopathic medicines. 5 Streptomycin and its salts.—General licences will be «^nted for the import of Streptomycin and its salts on a quota of 100 per cent, of half of best year's imports of this article from the dollar area.

6. Penicillin.—The licensing policy for all forms of bottled penicillin and its preparations is given beow: —

(A) General and Soft Currency licences will be panted on a quota of 150 per cent, of half of best vear's imports of the following varieties of bottled penicillin and its preparations from the respective areas. Licences will be valid for the import of these varieties only: —

(i) Crystalline Penicillin G. Calcium; fHE fcAzifitftt: 6 F INDIA EXTRAORDINARY 60? APPENDIX H—contd.

(ii) Procaine Penicillin G. with Crystalline Penicillin G. Oily injection;

(iii) Penicillin G. Diethul Aminoethy-1 easter Hydrodide;

(iv) Procaine Penicillin G. with Crystalline Penicillin G. buffered with aluminium monosterrate;

(v) Procaine Penicillin in oil;

(vi) Penicillin tablets;

(vii) Penicillin lozenges;

(viii) Penicillin ointments.

(B) General and Soft Currency licences for the three types of bottled penicillin mentioned below will be issued on a quota of 50 per cent, of half of best year's imports of these three varieties of bottled penicillin only from the respective areas.

(i) Crystalline Penicillin Sodium or Crystalline Penicillin Potassium;

(ii) Crystalline Penicillin Procaine;

(iii) Procaine Penicillin G. Fortified with Crystalline Penicillin G. (Sodium or Potassium) (Aqueous).

Licences granted under this quota will be valid for the import of the three varieties only mentioned above.

(C) Penicillin in bulk (excluding all forms of bottled Penicillin and its preparations) is allowed under Open General Licence (General) upto 30th September 1953.

7. Liver Extract Injectible.—General and soft currency licences for Liver Extract Injectible will be granted on the basis of a quota of 20 per cent, and 40 per cent, of half of best year's Imports of this article from the dollar area and soft currency area respectively.

8. Nikethamide.—Solt currency licences for Nikethamide will be granted on the basis of 33J per cent, of half of best year's imports.

The limiting factor for purposes of clearance of this article, will be both quantity and value.

9. AntiAeprosy drugs and P.A.S.—Soft currency licences for Antileprosy drugs (Sulphone Series of drugs) and P.A.S. and its Salts will be granted on a quota ol (J6§ per cent, and 5u per cent, respectively of half of best year's imports from soft currency area only.

10. Calcium Lactate— Soft currency licences will be granted for Calcium Lactate on the basis of 30 per cent, of half of best year's imports of this article.

11. Hydroxy Quinoline.—Soft currency Licences for Di-Iodo or Dichloro or Iodo-chloro Hydroxy Quinoline will be granted on a quota of 75 per cent, half of best year's imports from soft currency area of these articles only. Not more than 25 per cent, of the face value of the licence can be utilised for the import of Iodo-Chloro Hydroxy Quinoline.

THE GAZETTE OF itotA EXTRAORblNARY

[FAHT i APPENDIX H—contd.

12. Malt Extract, Santonin and Thymol.—Malt extract (excluding its preparations with Cod Liver Oil or other fish liver oils), Santonin and Thymol will be licensed freely from both dollar and soft currency areas. Although applications for these licences will be entertained from all categories of importers and licences granted liberally, it should not be taken for granted that licences will be issued to all applicants for any amount that may be applied for. In dealing with these applications the following considerations will be taken into account: —

(i) The applicant's financial ability to import in the half year the commodity upto the quantity and value applied for.

(ii) The applicant's previous connections with the particular drugs and medicines in question or drugs and'medicines in general.

(iii) Applications for unduly large amounts will be subject to strict scrutiny.

N.B.—Importers should in their own interests submit as much information as possible with the application in order to enable the licensing authorities to consider for what amount licences should be granted.

Importers applying for a licence for a sum exceeding one lakh of rupees should produce: —

(a) An undertaking on a stamped paper to the effect that he would (i) open immediately a confirmed irrevocable letter of credit for the amount applied for, if a licence were granted, and (ii) produce evidence regarding letter of credit to the licensing authority within a month of the date of issue of the licence, failing which the licence will be liable to be cancelled; or

(b) An Exchange Bank's letter to the effect that the requisite funds are available in the applicant's- account with the Bank and also that the exporters are willing to extend the requisite credit facilities in favour of the applicants.

Applicants should note that licences may be granted for a value less than the amount applied for.

Not more than one application may be made by the same applicant for each drug, during a licensing period.

13. Applications for "specialities" will also be considered subject to such conditions as the licensing authorities may decide to impose.

Applications for "specialities" should be made to the Chief Controller of Imports, New Delhi.

14. Applications from New Comers for Drugs and Medicines included in List I will be entertained. Such applications should be made in accordance with the procedure outlined in Section I of the Red Book.

SEC 1] APPENDIX H—contd.

List I The drugs and medicines listed below can be imported either in their pure form or as preparations thereof, containing one or more of the specified item in propKylactic or therapeutic quantities except where otherwise specified but excluding all B.P. tinctures and liquors.

The provisions of the Drugs Act, 1940 and the Rules thereunder should be complied with, wherever necessary: —

Acid Anthranilic excluding preparations thereof.

Acid Benzoic and Benzoates excluding preparations thereof. •Acid Gluconic excluding preparations thereof.

Acid Glycerophosphoric excluding preparations thereof.

Acid Mandelic.

Acetylcholine.

Acridines excluding Mepacrine and preparations containing Mepa* crine." Agar Agar excluding preparations thereof, Aletris root excluding preparations thereof.

[PART E APPENDIX L IMPORT OF DIAMONDS, PRECIOUS STONES, ETC. UNCUT AND UNSET FALLING, UNDER SERIAL NO. 61 OF PART V OF THE IMPORT TRADE CONTROL

SCHEDULE.

[PUBLIC NOTICE NO. 18 ITC(P.N.)/53, DATED

THE 22NDJANUARY 1953.] With a view to assist the Indian Lapidary Industry and to promote the export of goods manufactured or processed by that industry, established importers of unset and uncut diamonds will be granted import licences for the import of these goods from the Soft Currency area on the basis of a quota of 20 per cent, of half of best year's imports of diamonds unset and uncut during the basic period 1945-46 to 1951-52 subject to the condition that each licensee enters into a bond to export processed diamonds after cutting and polishing of the value equal to the imports within the following half year.

2. Import licences in subsequent licensing periods will be based on actual exports made in the preceding half year. Established importers who fall to carry out the terms of the bond mentioned in para 1 above will be disqualified from applying for import licences in the subsequent licensing periods.

3. In the case of rough synthetic stones uncut and unset falling under Serial No. 339 of Part IV and precious stones unset and imported uncut excluding diamonds falling under Serial No. 253 of.' Part IV, imports will be allowed on a more liberal basis. With effect from 1st January 1953, these goods will be permitted to be imported, upto 100 per cent, of tke rupee equivalent of the foreign exchange* received in payment of the F. O. B. value for the goods exported in. the preceding quarter.

4. The validity period of the licences issued for diamonds uncut and unset will be 6 months from the date of issue.

Applications for licences should be made in the prescribed form and manner to the licensing authorities at the ports.

Vases, metal SEC. la 623APPENDIX N IMPORT OF GARAGE TOOLS Licences will be granted for "Garage Tools" to importers who are established importers of both hardware falling under Serial No. 275 of Part IV and Motor Vehicle Spare Parts falling under Serial Numbers 293, 295 and 297 ,of Part IV of the Import Trade Control Schedule.

2. For this purpose such importers are advised to intimate to the Chief Controller of Imports, New Delhi in the form annexed the value of their quota licences for hardware as well as for Motor Vehicle Spare Parts obtained by them during the January—June 1952 licensing period for import from dollar and soft currency countries separately. The value for which licences can be granted will be intimated to them as soon as possible. An application in the prescribed form should then be made to the Hcensing authority at the port concerned.

3. Licences for import of garage tools will also be granted to the following categories of actual users for import either from the dollar area or from the soft currency area: —

(a) Recognised assemblers /manufacturers of automobiles.

(b) Garage Workshop employing not less than 50 workmen(both skilled and unskilled).

(c) Fleet Owners having 25 or more vehicles.

4. The information in the form annexed should be submitted by actual users alongwith certificates from the Development Wing of thf Ministry of Commerce & Industry in the case of assemblers and from Director of Industries or of Labour/State Transport Authority of the" State concerned as the case may be. Actual users will be advised of the allocations made to them and thereafter they should submit applications in the prescribed form to the licensing authority at the port concerned.

5. The information called for in paras. 2 and 4 above should be sent to the Chief Controller of Imports, New Delhi, by the 30th September 1953.

6. Licences issued for Garage tools will be valid only for the Import of the articles specified in the list attached.

APPENDIX P SUBJECT:—Licensing of Motor Vehicle Parts falling under S. Nos. 293, 295 and 297 of Part IV for the period July—December 1953.

Licences for motor-vehicle parts falling under S. Nos. 293, 295 and 297 of Part IV will be granted to established importers on the basis of a joint quota of past imports of all the articles falling under these S. Nos.

2. The quota for July-December 1953 will be 100 per cent, each of half of best year's imports from the dollar area and the soft currency area.

3. Those owning a fleet of 25 vehicles or above will be granted licences fot parts on the basis of Rs. 600 per petrol vehicle and Rs. 1000 per diesel vehicle.

4. Recognised assemblers will also be allowed licences on ad hoc basis. They should make their applications to the Chief Controller of Imports, New Delhi.

5. Firms, who have imported complete Motor Cycles or Scooters in the past can be granted licences for Motor Cycle parts on the basis of a quota of 10 per cent of half of best year's imports of complete Motor Cycles and Scooters, if they have no quota or a smaller quota for motor cycle parts.

6. It has also been decided to grant licences for Motor Vehicle parts to persons who have been dealing in spare parts of motorvehicles in the past and were obtaining their supplies through assemblers and other established importers provided they are prepared to invest a substantial sum for importing automobile parts during July—December 1953. Such persons in addition to producing the documents of turn-over prescribed for newccfmers should also produce a Bank Certificate to indicate their capacity to invest in the trade in the half year.

7. Licences for Motor Vehicle parts granted on the basis indicated in the preceding paragraphs will not be valid for imports of: —

(i) Bulb horns.

(ii) Piston rings below 6" dia. with the exception of (a) piston rings imported with the original equipment either C.K.D. or assembled, (b) piston rings for diesel engines, tractor engines or diesel motor vehicles (c) piston rings fitted to pistons when imported as complete piston assemblies, (d) elliptical rings for use on motor cycles, (e) Compensating rings and (f) expander type piston rings- or hydraulic piston rings or special engineered piston rings.

(iii) Fan belts for bottom width (i.e. on the inner diameter) more than 0-250 inch, and the following rubber parts: —

(1) Radiator hoses, (2) Hand and foot pump connections, (3) Rubber horn bulbs, (4) Rubber mats, (5) Gommet Windshield Wiper, (6) Rubber buffers—doors, (7) Rubber connection for filler tube—petrol tank, (8) Air vent pipe rubber connection—petrol tank, (9) Rubber LPART I APPENDIX P—contd. insulating washers fdr bolts connecting body and chassis, (10) Rubber connections—Air cleaner, (11) Rubber mountings for silencer, (12) Jointwasher—

Petrol filler tube with cap, (13) Rubber packing for battery clamping channel, (14) Sealing strips for trafficator boxes, (15) Sleeve rear drain pipe, (16) Rear strip roof opening, (17) Side strip roof opening, (18) Strip steering column, (19) Wind shield wiper tubing, and (20) Hose pipes other than brake hose pipes.

(iv) Kool cushions, Cushion covers, Steering wheel nut spinners, Visor Pouch, Mascots, Hood Ornaments, Fender Ornaments (illuminated and non-illuminated), Leather for Upholstery, spare cushions for seats whether made of cane, metal, cloth, plastic or any other material,

(v) Cab bodies, bus bodies, station wagon bodies, truck bodies, steel cabs for lorries, pick up bodies and Panel bodies.

(vi) Pistons,

(vii) Cylinder liners.

8. Not more than one per cent, of the face value of licences for motor vehicle parts can be utilised for the import of ball bearings not specified in Appendix 'C of this Book.

9. As regards (i) Pistons and (ii) cylinder liners both General and Soft Currency Licences will be granted on a quota of 75 per cent, and 100 per cent, of half of best year's imports of (a) Pistons and (b) Cylinder liners respectively from the area concerned. These licences, which will be specifically granted for the import of Pistons or Cylinder liners, will not be valid for the import of any other spare parts of Motor Vehicles.

SFC, 1] fi20 APPENDIX Q IMPORT OF SPECIAL ELECTRIC BULBS WHICH ARE FITTED TO PHOTOGRAPHIC INSTRUMENTS, SCIENTIFIC INSTRUMENTS AND OTHER INSTRUMENTS AND WHICH ARE IN A WAY PART OF SUCH INSTRUMENTS AND WITHOUT WHICH THE INSTRUMENTS BECOME UNSERVICEABLE

[PUBLIC NOTICE No. 50-ITC/(P.N.)/52, DATED THE 9TH MAY, 1952J.

Representations have been received that scientific, photographic and other instruments are often rendered idle on account of the fact that they need special types of electric bulbs for use with them and normally speaking the importers of such equipment do not hold import licences for electric bulbs. The following decisions have been taken to remove these difficulties: —

(a) In future, whenever a complete equipment is imported, in which an electric bulb having some peculiarity as to size, shape or filament is used, it will be permissible to import three spare bulbs of precisely the same type and make as is fitted to the equipment imported. If the number of bulbs fitted to the equipment exceeds one, the number of spare bulbs to be imported will be thnce the number of bulbs in the equipment. This concession will be applicable whether the equipment is imported against a licence or under Open General Licence, In the former case the value of the spare bulbs will be debited to the value of the licence against which the equipment is imported. In the latter case, the spare bulbs will be allowed clearance without a licence.

(b) In order to enable the supply of replacements to equipment already imported in the country in the past, importers holding licences for equipment of a type for which such special non-G.L.S. type lamps are required will be permitted to apply for the endorsement of their existing licences to cover the imports of special types of lamps.

This concession will only be granted to established importers who have imported equipment needing such bulbs during the last three years and it will be necessary for them to produce documents (bills of entry and Invoices) to show that they have made such Imports during each of the last three years. On production of such documents, their existing licences for the import of a particular equipment will be made valid for the import of spare bulbs also upto a value not exceeding 2£ per cent, of the total c.i.f. value of the licence.

(c) Where the equipment in question has been imported in the past under Open General Licence and continues to ™ported under the Open General Licence at present established Importers will be granted a special licence for the import of non-G.L.S. types of bulbs to fit such equipment equal m value to 2£ per cent, of the value of such equipment actually imported by them during any of the three years ending 31st March 1952 provided they produce documents to show that they have been making such imports during each of the last three years.

[PAHT I APPENDIX Q-rContd. 2 Applications for special licences or endorsements von existing licences in terms of the preceding paragraph should be made to the Import Trade Controllers at the ports with the relevant bills of entry, invoices, etc., and also a treasury chalan where a fresh licence is needed, in the form and manner prescribed in the Handbook. £tc. 1] APPENDIX R LICENSING POLICY FOB JULY—DECEMBER 1953—CHEMICALS.

Chemicals falling under Serial Numbers 22, 24, 27, 28, 29, 30 and 31 of Part V of the Import Trade Control Schedule are divided into two categories as follows: —

(a) Those included in O.G.L.

(b) Others not included in O.G.L.

The licensing policy for chemicals falling under category (b) will be as follows: —

1. General Licences.—(a) General licences will be granted only for the import of chemicals mentioned in List I, (a) on an individual basis as if each such chemical fell under a separate Serial Number of the Import Trade Control Schedule or (b) on the basis of a consolidated quota based on imports from dollar area of all chemicals falling under the above Serial Numbers with the exception of: —

(i) Caustic Soda;

(ii) Soda Ash; and

(iii) Chemicals specified in List I.

(b) Where licences are claimed on the basis of import of individual chemicals the quota percentages will be as shown against •each item in List I of half of best year's imports of that particular chemical during the basic year from dollar area and a licence valid for import of that particular chemical will be granted.

(c) In other cases licences will be granted on the basis of a consolidated quota of 20 per cent, of half of best year's imports from •dollar area of all chemicals falling under the above Serial Numbers •with the exception of: —

(i) Caustic Soda;

(ii) Soda Ash;

(iii) Chemicals specified in List I.

These quota licences will be valid for the import of only those •chemicals mentioned in List I, with the exception of Borax.

2. Soft currency licences.—(a) Soft currency licences will be granted for import of all chemicals (not included in the O.G.L.) with the exception of those mentioned in List II, (a) on an individual basis in respect of chemicals included in List III or (b) on the basis of a consolidated quota based on imports of all chemicals falling under the above mentioned Serial Numbers with the exception of: —

(i) Caustic Soda;

(ii) Soda Ash;

(iii) Chemicals included in List II and List III.

(b) Where quotas are claimed on an individual basis in respect of chemicals mentioned in List III the relevant quota percentages •will be as shown against each item in List III of half of best yew's imports of that particular chemical and a licence valid for import of that particular chemical will be granted-

[PART t APPENDIX R—contd.

(c) In other cases licences will be granted on the basis of a consolidated quota of 40 per cent, of half of the best year's imports, of all chemicals falling under the aforesaid Serial Numbers from Soft Currency countries with the exception of: —

(i) Caustic Soda;

(h) Soda Ash;

(iii) Chemicals included in List II and List III.

Licences issued under this quota will be valid for import of alt or any of the chemicals falling under aforesaid Serial Numbers with

[the following exceptions:,—

(i) Caustic Soda;

(ii) Soda Ash;

(iii) Thorium Nitrate;

(iv) Cerium Nitrate;

(v) Rare Earth Chloride;

(vi) Rare Earth Carbonates;

(vii) Specific Quota items in List III;

(viii) Prohibited items specified in List II.

3. Applications from Actual Users will be considered for the

[following chemicals:—•

(a) Acid Citric.

(b) Borax (for half of certified requirements only).

(c) Dicalcium Phosphate.

(d) Magnesium Carbonate light and magnesium insulating materials.

(e) Potassium Chlorate.

(f) Rubber Softeners.

(g) Synthetic Chemicals for manufacture of dyestuffs.

(h) X-Ray developers and fixing salts for the processing of X-Ray Films.

(i) Ionone 100 per cent.

(j) Triple pressed stearic acid for cosmetic manufacturers only.

(k) Pectin for fruit preservation industry.

(1) Insecticides, Fungicides and Weedicides.

(m) Any other chemical of a specialised nature or needed for a specific purpose for which full justification need be given.

4. It should be noted that the licences issued under this Public Notice will not be valid for the import of any chemical falling under S. No. I of Part III of the I.T.C. Schedule and the previous imports of those chemicals will not also be taken into account for purposes of calculation of qupta.

5. The licensing policy for Caustic Soda, Soda Ash and bleaching powder is given separately in Appendix 'A' to this Book.

APPENDIX X IMPORT OF SPARE PARTS OF A MAJOR ITEM AGAINST LICENCES ISSUED FOR

THE IMPORT OF MAJOR ITEM ONLY [PUBLIC NOTICE NO. 29-ITC(P.N.)/ 52, DATED THE 21ST MARCH, 1952 AS AMENDED BY PUBLIC NOTICE No. 62 ITC(P.N.)/53 DATED THE 11TH APRIL 1953.] The attention of importers is invited to the Ministry of Commerce Public Notice No. 184-ITC(P.N.)/51, dated the 19th November 1951, on the above subject. It has been represented that the exact scope of the concession regarding the import of spare parts against licences for the import of a major item is not quite clear and might lead to difficulties at the time of clearance of goods on arrival. It has therefore been decided in partial modification of the Public Notice under reference that the scheme will apply to specified items only.

2. For the present the scheme will be applicable tothe following articles: —

(1) Electrical instruments falling under Serial No. 39 of Part II of the Import Trade Control Schedule.

(2) Electrical instruments, apparatus and appliances specified in Serial Numbers 45 and 46 of Part II.

(3) Photographic instruments, apparatus and appliances falling under Serial No. 305 of Part IV.

(4) Electrical instruments, apparatus and appliances falling under Serial No. 78 of Part V.

(5) Instruments, apparatus and appliances} falling under Serial Nos. 92, 93 and 94 of Part V of the Import Trade Control Schedule.

(6) Industrial Sewing Machines falling under Serial No. 76 of Part V.

(7) Electrical Control gear and Electric Transmission gear falling under Serial No. 42 of Part II of the Import Trade Control Schedule.

(8) Conveyances, not otherwise specified, falling under Serial No. 86 of Part V of the Import Trade Control Schedule.

3. Spare parts for the above items even though classifiable under •different serial numbers of the Import -Trade Control Schedule will be allowed clearance up to 5 per cent, of the value of the.main licence provided that the parts are essential for the working of the' major article and have been given for that purpose some special shape or •quality which would not be essential for their use for any other purpose.

4. This concession is, however, subject to the condition, that no spare part which is for the time being on the prohibited, list may be imported.

5. The question of including other articles under the scope, of this concession is under examination and additions will be made from time to time. Representations from the trade and industry for the addition of any specific items to this scheme should ,be. addressed to the Chief Controller of Imports, Policy Branch, with such relevant data as will help in coming to a decision on the point.

N.B.—1. Where an actual user or an established importer having firm orders from an actual user desires to import any of the machines listed in this Appendix for own use, the application for an import licence will be considered on an ad hoc basis provided full justification for the requirement is given and it is proved that indigenous manufacturers are unable to fabricate the machmery required.

2. Where an actual user or an established importer having firm orders from an actual user desires to import spare parts of any of the machines listed in this Appendix the application for an import licence will be considered on merits, provided detailed reasons justifying the necessity for the imports of the articles applied for are furnished in the application. If the value of the spare parts required to be imported by an actual user having his place of business at (a) Howrah and Calcutta, (b) Madras, and (c) Bombay exceeds, in any half year, Rs. 5,000, the application should be accompanied by a certificate from a qualified Engineer, preferably on the staff of the applicant firm, to the effect that the spare parts required to be imported cannot be fabricated in any of the reputed workshops in (a) Howrah and Calcutta, (b) Madras, and (c) Bombay respectively.

3. Established importers having quota licences for spare parts of machinery falling under S. Nos. 36(5) of Part II, 4(5) of Part III and 65(5) of Part V, may apply for their licences being validated for the import of spares or any of the machines listed in this Appendix upto a value of Rs. 5,000 only. Applications for an endorsement to this effect should be made to the licensing authority concerned together with a detailed list of the parts desired to be imported and a certificate to the effect that these parts cannot be fabricated in any of the reputed workshops in (a) Howjah and Calcutta, (b) Madras and

(c) Bombay, as the case may be. ' —

APPENDIX Z IMPORT OF X-RAY ELECTROMEDICAL EQUIPMENT, ACCESSORIES AND SPARK PARTS OF ELECTROMEDICAL APPARATUS ETC. BY THE SUPPLIERS or X-RAY AND ELECTROMEDICAL EQUIPMENT.

It has been represented to the Government of India that although electromedical apparatus is allowed to be imported liberally, the suppliers of these apparatuses cannot get spare parts, accessories and supplies of allied nature for servicing and maintaining the existing equipments properly as these accessories, spare parts and articles of allied nature were classified under different Serial Nos. of the Import Trade Control Schedule which are sometimes licensable strictly on a quota basis or are not licensed at all.

2. With a view to maintain the operation of the X-Ray and electromedical equipment, it has been decided that the suppliers of X-ray .and electromedical equipment can apply for their requirements of various articles although they may be falling under different Serial Nos. of the Import Trade Control Schedule under One application. A list of articles desired to be imported in broad categories like rotary converters, transformers, photographic tanks, transformer oil and film hangers etc. and spare parts of the equipment should all be listed and a consolidated application should be made for all these. The requirements should be certified by the All India-X-ray and Electromedical Traders' Association, Bombay.

3. On receipt ,pf the consolidated application giving a list of the articles desired to be imported and the value of each duly certified by the Association, the Import Trade Controller at the port will grant each firm a licence for its consolidated requirements and attach the list of the articles with the licence. Where certain articles are licensed on a quota basis the reasonableness of the demand of those articles can be judged from the firm's quota in respect of that article.

The quota for these articles will be merged in the licence allowed under this Public Notice. If the firms concerned have obtained any quota licence they should mention the name of the article and the amount of quota licence allowed so that adjustments for the same can be made, if necessary.

4. Although the value of individual articles as per requirements certified by the Association will be indicated in the list of articles for which the licence is granted, the licence holders can exceed the value of individual items by 25 per cent, of the value for that item provided that the total c.i.f. value of the licence is not exceeded.

5. It has also been decided that the import of one pair each of lead rubber X-ray apron, lead rubber gloves (required for the radiologist) and lead glass "goggles" may be allowed clearance under the O.G.L. when they are imported alone with the X-Rnv pnninmonf v,n+ ™f separated from it ,572 THE* GAZETTE OF INf)IA EXTRAORDINARY

[PAHT 1 APPENDIX ZA SCHEME FOR LICENSING IMPORT OF IRRIGATION PROJECT EQUIPMENT.

The following decisions of the Government of India in regard to the issue of licences for Machinery and Equipment required by various Irrigation Project under the Irrigation Project Equipment Scheme are hereby announced for general information.

2. Subject to the provisions of paragraph 3 below the Irrigation Project Equipment Scheme snail only apply to the following goods ^classifiable under Parts I, II and V of the Import Trade Control Schedule: —

Serial No. 17.—Cast iron and Steel Valves and similar controls for waterworks, Irrigation and Hydro Electric Scheme.

Serial No. 20.—Fabricated Gates for Dams and Barrages.

Serial No. 9.—Iron and Steel articles and controls for Dams and Barrages also cocks & Taps.

Serial No. 36.—All goods included in Serial No. 36(1), 36(2), 36(3), 36(4) and 36(5)—required for Irrigation HydroElectrical Schemes. ' Serial No. 65.—All goods falling under Serial No. 65(1), 65(2), 65(3), 65(4) and 65(5) when required for Irrigation Projects.

Serial JVo. 92.—Water Meters and Measuring Instruments required for Water Works, Irrigation and Hydro-Electric Projects.

3. The Scheme will apply to applications of an aggregate value of Rs. 25,000 or over for any project or subsidiary thereto.

4. These requirements will be centrally licensed by the Chief Controller of Imports in New Delhi and all the requirements should be put in one application rather than piecemeal according to the Serial Number of the Import Trade Control Schedule. The applications will* be treated in the same way as the applications for Capital Goods. They should be made in the form prescribed for Capital Goods and Heavy Electrical Plant and should be submitted in duplicate to Chief Controller of Imports through. Central Water and Power Commission, New Delhi.

5. The initial period of validity for these licences will be 1 year from the date of issue and will be extended to a maximum period of 3 years on production of documentary evidence to show that a firm order has been placed and accepted by foreign suppliers. In cases where such evidence is produced at the time of submission of the application, licences will be issued with 'the maximum period of validity and no further extension will be necessary,

6. The applications, should be accompanied with the usual treasury receipt and the Income-tax Verification Certificate Number or the .specific exemption number as in the case of Capital Goods applications.

ANKEXURE (1) Quota licences for tractors, Rotary Hoes and Tillers will not be' valid for:—

(i) makes other than those imported by the importers concerned in the past;

(ii) for tractors etc. having a H.P. of less than 15 at the drawbar;

(iii) for those operated by petrol or gasoline.

2. Quota licences will be subject to the further conditions that the importers concerned—

(i) continue as accredited agents of the manufacturers;

(ii) maintain facilities for servicing of the tractors imported by them to the satisfaction of the Ministry of Food & Agriculture; and

(iii) import spare parts of a value not less than 15 per cent, of the value of the tractors Rotary Hoes and Tillersactually imported against their licences.

3. The importation of spare parts of agricultural tractors falling, under Serial No. 74 of Part V of the Import Trade Control Schedule is covered by Open General Licence No. XXIX. (It should be noted that items which are specifically mentio'ned under a separate Serial No. in the Import Trade Control Schedule, as for example Ball Bearings, tyres and tubes cannot be imported against this item. However, not more than 1% of the face value of quota licences issued for tractors may be utilised for the import of ball bearings of the types not specified in Appendix C to this Book).

Difficulty has, however, been experienced by importers in clearing consignments against Open General Licence No. XXIX of interchangeable spare parts which can be used for both tractors and motor vehicles. To overcome this difficulty, licences by way of identification certificates will be issued on the recommendation of the Ministry of Food and Agriculture to established importers and others whomaintain regular servicing organisations. On the presentation of these licences (identification certificates) the Customs authoritieswill allow clearance of interchangeable spare parts, without any value limit, against Open General Licence No. XXIX.

4. Importers of tractors will also be granted licences for the import of the following articles on the basis indicated against each:—

(a) Tractor service tools falling under Serial No. 275 of Part IV upto 10 per cent, of half of best year's imports of tractors;

(b) Tractor trailers upto 20 per cent, by number of the actual number of tractors imported during the best year.

(c) Tractor attachments upto 30 per cent, of half of best year'simports of tractors. The list of the attachments for which licences will be valid is given in Annexure (2).

(d) Spare parts of tractor trailers and tractor attachmentsspecified in Annexure (2) on the basis of 2i per cent, of half of best year's imports of tractors.

Sir. 1] 57T APPENDIX ZB—contd.

ANNEXURE (1)—contd. t 5. Applications for the import of workshop tools and equipment, by approved importers of tractors will also be considered on merits.

The following information should be submitted along with theapplication:—

(i) Number of tractors imported during the last 3 years;

(ii) Location of workshops already established indicating details of the machinery installed;

(iii) Details of the new workshop machinery proposed to be imported;

(iv) Whether the machinery referred to under (iii) above isto be installed in one of the existing workshops or a new workshop is proposed to be established.

6. Applications by non-established importers and by established' importers for new makes of tractors, Rotary hoes and Tillers not imported by them in the past will be considered, ad hoc, on merits.

Such applications should be accompanied by the following additional information:—

(i) Whether they are accredited agents of any manufacturer.

Name of the machine and name of manufacturer should' be furnished alongwith evidence of sole agency i.e., agency agreement in original;

(ii) Whether they have adequate workshop facilities and trained engineers for servicing. If so, full particularsof the available servicing facilities should be given duly certified by the Agricultural Department of the StateGovernment concerned;

(iii) Whether they have sufficient capacity to import tractors, Rotary hoes, tillers, implements, spare parts etc., to the full extent: Bank Certificate to show this should be furnished;

(iv) Whether the tractors etc. desired to be imported arecovered by a Nebraska Test Certificate or by a certificate from the National Institute of Agriculture Engineering or by the official certificate of the Government concerned confirming the manufacturer's specifications.
